### OmniVox: Zero-Shot Emotion Recognition with Omni-LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21480v1)
- **Authors**: John Murzaku, Owen Rambow
- **Abstract**: The use of omni-LLMs (large language models that accept any modality as input), particularly for multimodal cognitive state tasks involving speech, is understudied. We present OmniVox, the first systematic evaluation of four omni-LLMs on the zero-shot emotion recognition task. We evaluate on two widely used multimodal emotion benchmarks: IEMOCAP and MELD, and find zero-shot omni-LLMs outperform or are competitive with fine-tuned audio models. Alongside our audio-only evaluation, we also evaluate omni-LLMs on text only and text and audio. We present acoustic prompting, an audio-specific prompting strategy for omni-LLMs which focuses on acoustic feature analysis, conversation context analysis, and step-by-step reasoning. We compare our acoustic prompting to minimal prompting and full chain-of-thought prompting techniques. We perform a context window analysis on IEMOCAP and MELD, and find that using context helps, especially on IEMOCAP. We conclude with an error analysis on the generated acoustic reasoning outputs from the omni-LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OmniVox, a framework for zero-shot emotion recognition in conversations (ERC) using omni-LLMs. It systematically evaluates the performance of four omni-LLMs (Gemini, Gemini Lite, GPT-4o, and Phi-4) on the IEMOCAP and MELD datasets, focusing primarily on audio input. The authors explore various prompting strategies (minimal, acoustic, chain-of-thought), analyze the impact of context window size, and conduct a detailed error analysis, examining acoustic feature misperceptions.  The key finding is that omni-LLMs show promising, sometimes surpassing fine-tuned audio models, demonstrating the potential of these models for zero-shot multimodal cognitive state tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its **systematic exploration** of omni-LLMs for zero-shot audio-based emotion recognition. While previous work has explored zero-shot ERC with text and some use of LLMs, this paper provides the first comprehensive analysis of omni-LLMs' audio understanding capabilities for this specific task and the investigation of prompt engineering techniques tailored for this modality. The acoustic prompting strategy is a novel contribution. However, the use of IEMOCAP and MELD datasets, common evaluation metrics (W-F1), and chain-of-thought prompting are standard practices, reducing the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from demonstrating the **potential of omni-LLMs for complex tasks like emotion recognition without task-specific fine-tuning**. This finding is important because it suggests that these models possess emergent audio understanding skills. Furthermore, the error analysis offers insights into the limitations of current omni-LLMs regarding acoustic feature perception, which points towards potential avenues for future research. However, the performance gains, while present, are relatively modest compared to state-of-the-art fine-tuned models. Also, the reliance on closed-source models (GPT-4o, Gemini) limits the reproducibility and generalizability of the results. The impact on the field is therefore somewhat constrained.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Systematic Evaluation:** The paper conducts a well-designed set of experiments across multiple models, datasets, and prompting strategies.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The inclusion of context window analysis and acoustic feature analysis offers valuable insights.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Closed-Source Models:** The reliance on closed-source models hinders reproducibility.
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** Evaluation is limited to two datasets, which might not generalize to other conversational emotion recognition scenarios.
    *   **Incremental Improvements:** The performance gains over existing audio models, while present, aren't dramatically large.
    *   **Lack of Ablation Studies:** The impact of various components (e.g., specific acoustic features) within the acoustic prompting strategy isn't deeply analyzed through ablation.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper's influence lies in motivating further research on:
    * Prompt engineering for audio-based tasks with LLMs
    * Improving audio understanding and representation in LLMs
    * Developing open-source omni-LLMs with strong audio capabilities

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid, novel contribution by systematically exploring zero-shot emotion recognition with omni-LLMs, especially focusing on audio. The analyses and discussion provide insights into the strengths and weaknesses of these models. However, the reliance on closed-source models, relatively incremental performance improvements, and reliance on standard datasets limits the impact. While a useful contribution, it's not a breakthrough result.

### Invert2Restore: Zero-Shot Degradation-Blind Image Restoration
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21486v1)
- **Authors**: Hamadi Chihaoui, Paolo Favaro
- **Abstract**: Two of the main challenges of image restoration in real-world scenarios are the accurate characterization of an image prior and the precise modeling of the image degradation operator. Pre-trained diffusion models have been very successfully used as image priors in zero-shot image restoration methods. However, how to best handle the degradation operator is still an open problem. In real-world data, methods that rely on specific parametric assumptions about the degradation model often face limitations in their applicability. To address this, we introduce Invert2Restore, a zero-shot, training-free method that operates in both fully blind and partially blind settings -- requiring no prior knowledge of the degradation model or only partial knowledge of its parametric form without known parameters. Despite this, Invert2Restore achieves high-fidelity results and generalizes well across various types of image degradation. It leverages a pre-trained diffusion model as a deterministic mapping between normal samples and undistorted image samples. The key insight is that the input noise mapped by a diffusion model to a degraded image lies in a low-probability density region of the standard normal distribution. Thus, we can restore the degraded image by carefully guiding its input noise toward a higher-density region. We experimentally validate Invert2Restore across several image restoration tasks, demonstrating that it achieves state-of-the-art performance in scenarios where the degradation operator is either unknown or partially known.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Invert2Restore (I2R), a novel zero-shot, training-free image restoration method. I2R addresses the challenge of image restoration in real-world scenarios where the degradation operator is either completely unknown (fully blind) or only partially known (partially blind).  I2R leverages a pre-trained Denoising Diffusion Implicit Model (DDIM) as a deterministic mapping between normal noise samples and clean images. The core idea is that the noise corresponding to a degraded image, when mapped through the inverted DDIM, resides in a low-probability region of the standard normal distribution. I2R then "restores" the image by guiding this noise towards a higher density region of the standard normal distribution. This is achieved by identifying and correcting local noise patches that deviate from normality, and then transforming back to image space via the DDIM.  The paper demonstrates I2R's effectiveness across a variety of image restoration tasks, including JPEG de-artifacting, deraining, deblurring, and super-resolution, and shows it achieves state-of-the-art performance in many scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of operating in noise space rather than image space for restoration is a fresh perspective, particularly in the context of diffusion models. While diffusion models have been used for image restoration before, the approach of inverting to the noise space and then "rectifying" that noise, guided by statistical properties of the normal distribution, is quite novel. Most existing works address fully/partially blind image restoration by either estimating kernel parameters, or by modifying the DDIM reverse process, making I2R significantly distinct.

*   **Significance:** The ability to perform effective image restoration *without* task-specific training, and *without* needing to know the explicit degradation model, is a significant advancement. It expands the applicability of image restoration techniques to real-world scenarios where degradation models are often unknown or complex. The experimental results appear strong, demonstrating consistent state-of-the-art performance, or at least competitive performance, across a diverse set of degradation types. The method's ability to work even in scenarios where previous approaches require explicit kernel estimation, demonstrates high practical value.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Training-free and zero-shot:** Eliminates the need for costly datasets and retraining for new degradation types.
    *   **Handles fully blind and partially blind scenarios:** Significantly increases applicability compared to methods requiring full knowledge of the degradation model.
    *   **Novel approach of noise space rectification:** A unique perspective that shows promise in handling complex degradations.
    *   **Strong empirical results:**  Demonstrates good quantitative and qualitative performance on a variety of image restoration tasks.
    *   **Ablation study:** Systematically examines the importance of different components of the method.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational cost:** While the paper touches on efficiency, diffusion models are inherently computationally intensive. While the method has reasonable computation time, it is still significant, and improvement would increase practical adoption.
    *   **Reliance on a strong pre-trained diffusion model:**  The performance is heavily dependent on the quality and generalization ability of the pre-trained DDIM. The models they rely on can sometimes exhibit specific failure modes or biases which impact reconstruction quality.
    *   **Statistical test parameters tuning:** Though claimed as automatic by using normality tests, there might be situations where the chosen p-value requires specific tuning based on the degradation type, diminishing its generality.
    *   **Limited Analysis on Failure Modes:** Despite the strong results, there is little discussion in the paper on *why* this method works, or the conditions in which the model fails to perform well.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   The work can spur further research into using noise space manipulations for image restoration and other inverse problems with diffusion models.
    *   The ability to handle unknown degradations makes it a potentially valuable tool for real-world applications like historical document restoration or medical image enhancement.
    *   It may prompt the development of more robust statistical tests tailored to the characteristics of noise spaces in diffusion models.

Overall, the paper makes a novel and significant contribution to the field of image restoration. The method is well-motivated, thoroughly evaluated, and demonstrates strong practical potential.

### Keyword-Oriented Multimodal Modeling for Euphemism Identification
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21504v1)
- **Authors**: Yuxue Hu, Junsong Li, Meixuan Chen, Dongyu Su, Tongguan Wang, Ying Sha
- **Abstract**: Euphemism identification deciphers the true meaning of euphemisms, such as linking "weed" (euphemism) to "marijuana" (target keyword) in illicit texts, aiding content moderation and combating underground markets. While existing methods are primarily text-based, the rise of social media highlights the need for multimodal analysis, incorporating text, images, and audio. However, the lack of multimodal datasets for euphemisms limits further research. To address this, we regard euphemisms and their corresponding target keywords as keywords and first introduce a keyword-oriented multimodal corpus of euphemisms (KOM-Euph), involving three datasets (Drug, Weapon, and Sexuality), including text, images, and speech. We further propose a keyword-oriented multimodal euphemism identification method (KOM-EI), which uses cross-modal feature alignment and dynamic fusion modules to explicitly utilize the visual and audio features of the keywords for efficient euphemism identification. Extensive experiments demonstrate that KOM-EI outperforms state-of-the-art models and large language models, and show the importance of our multimodal datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Keyword-Oriented Multimodal Modeling for Euphemism Identification":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of euphemism identification, which is crucial for content moderation and combating illicit activities online.  Existing methods are primarily text-based, but the authors argue that multimodal analysis (text, images, and audio) is necessary due to the increasing use of euphemisms in social media contexts where these modalities are present.  To address the lack of suitable datasets, the authors introduce a new keyword-oriented multimodal euphemism corpus (KOM-Euph) consisting of text, image, and speech triplets in three domains: Drug, Weapon, and Sexuality. They also propose a keyword-oriented multimodal euphemism identification method (KOM-EI) that uses cross-modal feature alignment and dynamic fusion to leverage visual and audio information.  Experiments demonstrate that KOM-EI outperforms state-of-the-art text-based models and large language models, highlighting the importance of multimodal data in this task.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper has good novelty. The most significant contribution is the introduction of the KOM-Euph dataset. The recognition that multimodal data is crucial for euphemism detection in modern online environments is insightful. The KOM-EI model, while building on existing techniques like BERT and CLIP, presents a novel way to combine these models with feature alignment and dynamic fusion specifically for euphemism identification.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses an important problem with real-world implications. Improved euphemism identification can help combat illicit activities like drug trafficking and hate speech online. The KOM-Euph dataset is a valuable resource for the research community and could stimulate further work in this area. The KOM-EI model demonstrates that multimodal approaches can significantly improve accuracy compared to text-based methods.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Dataset Contribution:** The creation and release of the KOM-Euph dataset are a strong point. The lack of such resources has been a limiting factor in multimodal euphemism research.
    *   **Multimodal Approach:**  The KOM-EI model effectively integrates textual, visual, and audio features, demonstrating the benefits of multimodal analysis.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly defines the problem of euphemism identification and provides a well-motivated solution.
    *   **Experimental Results:** The experimental results convincingly show that KOM-EI outperforms state-of-the-art baselines and large language models.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies provide insights into the importance of different model components and modalities.
    *   **Cost Efficiency**: The computational efficiency makes it a valuable tool to leverage
    *   **Analysis of LLM performance**: The analysis with LLM performance on the task highlights some of the difficulties and deficiencies that are worth pointing out

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Dataset Size:** While KOM-Euph is a valuable resource, the overall size of the dataset could be larger. Increasing the size and diversity of the dataset could further improve the performance of multimodal models.
    *   **Dataset Bias:**  The dataset is derived from Reddit, Gab, and Slangpedia, which may introduce biases. The authors should address potential biases in the dataset and their impact on model performance.
    *   **Model Complexity:** While effective, the KOM-EI model is relatively complex and involves several components (BERT, CLIP, Wav2Vec 2.0, feature alignment, dynamic fusion). The authors could explore simpler or more lightweight models that achieve comparable performance.
    *   **The analysis with LLM performance on the task highlights some of the difficulties and deficiencies that are worth pointing out however it would be interesting to see it deployed in a more realistic settings where the answers are not given but are also part of the model reasoning

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research on euphemism identification and multimodal content moderation. The KOM-Euph dataset could become a standard benchmark for evaluating multimodal models in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes valuable contributions to the field of euphemism identification by addressing a critical gap in multimodal data and proposing an effective multimodal model. The strengths of the paper, particularly the dataset contribution and the demonstrable improvements in performance, outweigh the weaknesses. While the dataset size could be larger and the model is somewhat complex, the overall novelty and significance of the work justify a solid score. It's not ground breaking research, but a significant step in pushing the boundary and calling attention to something not being addressed with the same detail.
Score: 7

### Combining Artificial Users and Psychotherapist Assessment to Evaluate Large Language Model-based Mental Health Chatbots
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21540v1)
- **Authors**: Florian Onur Kuhlmeier, Leon Hanschmann, Melina Rabe, Stefan Luettke, Eva-Lotta Brakemeier, Alexander Maedche
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) promise to overcome limitations of rule-based mental health chatbots through more natural conversations. However, evaluating LLM-based mental health chatbots presents a significant challenge: Their probabilistic nature requires comprehensive testing to ensure therapeutic quality, yet conducting such evaluations with people with depression would impose an additional burden on vulnerable people and risk exposing them to potentially harmful content. Our paper presents an evaluation approach for LLM-based mental health chatbots that combines dialogue generation with artificial users and dialogue evaluation by psychotherapists. We developed artificial users based on patient vignettes, systematically varying characteristics such as depression severity, personality traits, and attitudes toward chatbots, and let them interact with a LLM-based behavioral activation chatbot. Ten psychotherapists evaluated 48 randomly selected dialogues using standardized rating scales to assess the quality of behavioral activation and its therapeutic capabilities. We found that while artificial users showed moderate authenticity, they enabled comprehensive testing across different users. In addition, the chatbot demonstrated promising capabilities in delivering behavioral activation and maintaining safety. Furthermore, we identified deficits, such as ensuring the appropriateness of the activity plan, which reveals necessary improvements for the chatbot. Our framework provides an effective method for evaluating LLM-based mental health chatbots while protecting vulnerable people during the evaluation process. Future research should improve the authenticity of artificial users and develop LLM-augmented evaluation tools to make psychotherapist evaluation more efficient, and thus further advance the evaluation of LLM-based mental health chatbots.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents an evaluation framework for Large Language Model (LLM)-based mental health chatbots. It addresses the challenge of assessing the therapeutic quality and safety of these chatbots without exposing vulnerable individuals to potential harm. The framework combines dialogue generation using artificial users with psychotherapist assessments of the resulting dialogues. These artificial users are based on patient vignettes with systematically varied characteristics (depression severity, personality traits, and attitudes toward chatbots).  Ten psychotherapists evaluated dialogues between the chatbot and 48 artificial users, using standardized rating scales to assess behavioral activation quality and therapeutic capabilities. The study identifies shortcomings in the chatbot (e.g., ensuring appropriate activity plans, responsiveness), suggests improvements, and demonstrates the feasibility of artificial users for comprehensive testing. The authors claim the approach offers a risk-free method for evaluating LLM-based mental health chatbots while protecting vulnerable individuals.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty and Significance:*

The paper introduces a reasonably novel approach to evaluating mental health chatbots, particularly in the context of LLMs. Combining artificial users with expert psychotherapist assessment addresses a crucial gap in ensuring the safety and efficacy of these interventions. The systematic variation of artificial user characteristics is a significant strength, allowing for testing across a more diverse range of user profiles compared to previous studies. While the concept of artificial users isn't entirely new (Chen et al., 2023 is cited), this paper expands upon it by incorporating richer user profiles, focusing on therapeutic (rather than diagnostic) conversations, and eliciting detailed feedback from experts.

*Strengths:*

*   **Addresses an Important Problem:** The paper directly tackles the ethical and practical challenges of evaluating potentially harmful mental health chatbots.
*   **Systematic Approach:**  The methodology is well-structured, from the creation of artificial users based on clinically relevant vignettes to the use of standardized rating scales by trained psychotherapists.
*   **Detailed Evaluation:** The paper provides a detailed analysis of the chatbot's performance, identifying specific strengths and weaknesses in different phases of the behavioral activation protocol.
*   **Actionable Insights:** The study goes beyond simply identifying problems; it offers concrete suggestions for improving the chatbot's design and functionality.
*   **Mixed-Methods Approach:** Combining quantitative ratings with qualitative feedback from experts provides a comprehensive understanding of the chatbot's capabilities and limitations.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Limited Authenticity of Artificial Users:** The psychotherapists noted that the artificial users exhibited unrealistically high compliance and engagement, a limitation also observed in prior research. This raises concerns about the generalizability of the findings to real-world users. The reliance on GPT-4 to generate user responses could limit the expression of genuine human variance, and the user attitudes were simplistic (binary: high/low).
*   **Small Sample Size of Psychotherapists:** With only 10 participants, the results may not be representative of all psychotherapists. There is a risk of bias due to the reliance on a convenience sample from the research team's network. Also, the dialogues each psychotherapist evaluates is relatively small (3-6 dialogues).
*   **Focus on First Session:** The evaluation is limited to the first session of behavioral activation.  The chatbot's performance in subsequent sessions and its ability to maintain therapeutic progress over time remain unexplored.
*   **Lack of Statistical Significance:**  The lack of statistically significant differences in chatbot quality based on depression severity is a minor weakness. The authors acknowledge the limited power due to the sample size, but it highlights the need for larger studies.
*   **Resource Intensive:** The authors acknowledge their evaluation is resource intensive, raising questions of scalability.

*Potential Influence:*

The paper offers a valuable framework that can be adopted by other researchers and developers in the field of mental health chatbots. It emphasizes the importance of rigorous testing and expert evaluation in ensuring the safety and efficacy of these interventions.  It also underscores the need for more realistic artificial users and more scalable evaluation methods. The identified limitations of the chatbot provide a roadmap for future improvements.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field, but there are significant weaknesses. The approach of combining artificial users with expert assessment is valuable, and the systematic nature of the study is commendable. However, the limited authenticity of the artificial users and the small sample size of psychotherapists significantly impact the generalizability of the findings. The limited exploration of long term interactions and scalability also reduce the impact. Despite these limitations, the study provides a practical framework, actionable insights, and a pathway for future research.

### LOCATEdit: Graph Laplacian Optimized Cross Attention for Localized Text-Guided Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21541v1)
- **Authors**: Achint Soni, Meet Soni, Sirisha Rambhatla
- **Abstract**: Text-guided image editing aims to modify specific regions of an image according to natural language instructions while maintaining the general structure and the background fidelity. Existing methods utilize masks derived from cross-attention maps generated from diffusion models to identify the target regions for modification. However, since cross-attention mechanisms focus on semantic relevance, they struggle to maintain the image integrity. As a result, these methods often lack spatial consistency, leading to editing artifacts and distortions. In this work, we address these limitations and introduce LOCATEdit, which enhances cross-attention maps through a graph-based approach utilizing self-attention-derived patch relationships to maintain smooth, coherent attention across image regions, ensuring that alterations are limited to the designated items while retaining the surrounding structure. \method consistently and substantially outperforms existing baselines on PIE-Bench, demonstrating its state-of-the-art performance and effectiveness on various editing tasks. Code can be found on https://github.com/LOCATEdit/LOCATEdit/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LOCATEdit paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "LOCATEdit: Graph Laplacian Optimized Cross Attention for Localized Text-Guided Image Editing" addresses the problem of imprecise control in text-guided image editing. Existing methods rely on masks derived from cross-attention maps, which often lead to edits spilling over into unintended regions, causing artifacts and distortions. LOCATEdit introduces a novel approach that enhances cross-attention maps using a graph-based method. It constructs CASA graphs (Cross and Self-Attention graphs) that encode cross-attention maps as nodes and self-attention relationships as weighted edges. A graph Laplacian regularizer is applied to these graphs to enforce spatial consistency, ensuring smoother and more coherent attention across image regions.  The method also incorporates an IP-Adapter and selective pruning on text embeddings to further refine the editing process. The paper claims LOCATEdit consistently outperforms existing baselines on the PIE-Bench dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the use of a graph Laplacian regularization on CASA graphs derived from cross- and self-attention maps for localized text-guided image editing. Combining cross-attention and self-attention and then utilizing the resulting graph structure to improve editing precision is a reasonable idea that shows originality. Other parts of the framework involve utilizing IP-adapters and selective pruning of text embeddings to suppress noise.

*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is significant. Precise control in text-guided image editing is crucial for practical applications. The paper presents a potentially useful technique for reducing artifacts and improving spatial consistency, which are limitations of existing methods.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The CASA graph construction and Laplacian regularization are technically sound and well-motivated.
    *   The method is training-free, which is desirable as it avoids the need for extensive training datasets.
    *   The closed-form solution for the optimization problem is efficient.
    *   The experiments on PIE-Bench demonstrate improvements over existing baselines, providing empirical evidence of the method's effectiveness. The qualitative results visually confirm the benefits of the approach.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the integration of Laplacian regularization within the cross-attention mechanism constitutes a solid idea, it may be deemed incremental.
    *   The use of IP-Adapter and selective pruning may reduce the impact of contributions to the core idea.
    *   The reliance on self-attention could introduce biases or dependencies on the initial image structure. The method could struggle with images that have ambiguous self-attention relationships.
    *   The experimental evaluation is primarily on the PIE-Bench dataset. While PIE-Bench is a good starting point, further evaluation on more diverse and challenging datasets would strengthen the paper.
    *   While the paper claims the approach disentangles the changes to the localized areas, further analysis is needed to quantitatively analyze this effect.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the claims are robust, the paper has the potential to influence future research in text-guided image editing. The graph Laplacian regularization approach could be adopted and extended by other researchers. The method's training-free nature makes it more accessible and adaptable. However, the magnitude of the influence will depend on how well the method generalizes to other datasets and editing tasks.

*   **Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

LOCATEdit addresses an important problem in text-guided image editing and introduces a novel technique with empirical support. The use of CASA graphs and Laplacian regularization is a good idea to make the editing process precise. However, the novelty may be viewed as incremental. Also, a more comprehensive evaluation with various benchmarks and rigorous quantitative disentangling analysis may enhance the value of the work. Overall, the paper offers a valuable contribution to the field, meriting a score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### SWI: Speaking with Intent in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21544v1)
- **Authors**: Yuwei Yin, EunJeong Hwang, Giuseppe Carenini
- **Abstract**: Intent, typically clearly formulated and planned, functions as a cognitive framework for reasoning and problem-solving. This paper introduces the concept of Speaking with Intent (SWI) in large language models (LLMs), where the explicitly generated intent encapsulates the model's underlying intention and provides high-level planning to guide subsequent analysis and communication. By emulating deliberate and purposeful thoughts in the human mind, SWI is hypothesized to enhance the reasoning capabilities and generation quality of LLMs. Extensive experiments on mathematical reasoning benchmarks consistently demonstrate the superiority of Speaking with Intent over Baseline (i.e., generation without explicit intent). Moreover, SWI outperforms answer-trigger prompting methods Chain-of-Thought and Plan-and-Solve and maintains competitive performance with the strong method ARR (Analyzing, Retrieving, and Reasoning). Additionally, the effectiveness and generalizability of SWI are solidified on reasoning-intensive question answering (QA) and text summarization benchmarks, where SWI brings consistent improvement to the Baseline generation. In text summarization, SWI-generated summaries exhibit greater accuracy, conciseness, and factual correctness, with fewer hallucinations. Furthermore, human evaluations verify the coherence, effectiveness, and interpretability of the intent produced by SWI. This proof-of-concept study creates a novel avenue for enhancing LLMs' reasoning abilities with cognitive notions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SWI: Speaking with Intent in Large Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Speaking with Intent" (SWI), a novel approach to enhance the reasoning and generation capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). SWI involves LLMs explicitly generating a statement of intent *before* each step of analysis and reasoning. This intent acts as a high-level plan that guides subsequent processing. The authors hypothesize that this mirrors human cognition, leading to improved reasoning. They evaluate SWI on mathematical reasoning, multiple-choice question answering, and text summarization benchmarks, comparing it to baselines and established methods like Chain-of-Thought (CoT), Plan-and-Solve, and Analyzing, Retrieving, and Reasoning (ARR). The results show that SWI consistently improves over the baseline, surpassing CoT and competing well with ARR, particularly on mathematical reasoning. Human evaluations confirm the coherence, effectiveness, and interpretability of the generated intents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The concept of explicitly generating an intent statement within an LLM's reasoning loop is a significant contribution. While intent modeling and planning have been explored in related fields (intent detection and intent-driven generation), the novel aspect lies in making the *LLM itself* articulate its own intent at each step. This mimics a metacognitive process, which hasn't been extensively explored in the same manner for LLMs. This iterative approach, in contrast to one-shot planning, is another layer of originality.

*   **Significance:** The improved performance across diverse tasks, particularly the competitive results with ARR, highlights the potential of SWI. It provides evidence that incorporating cognitive-inspired mechanisms can enhance LLM reasoning and generation. The improvements in text summarization, specifically the reduction in hallucinations, address a crucial challenge in LLM deployment. The human evaluations further reinforce the practicality and understanding of this approach. The concept introduces a shift towards empowering LLMs to be more transparent and controllable in their reasoning process.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Concept:** The SWI concept is clearly articulated and easy to grasp.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation covers diverse tasks and benchmarks, strengthening the claims of generalizability.
    *   **Strong Results:** The empirical results consistently demonstrate the benefits of SWI.
    *   **Human Evaluation:** Including human evaluation adds valuable qualitative insights.
    *   **Comparison to Baselines:** The paper not only benchmarks against a baseline but also compares against other established and strong methods like CoT and ARR.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement Over ARR:** While competitive with ARR, the paper doesn't always show substantial improvements on mathematical reasoning. The paper acknowledges this competitiveness but doesn't provide a very detailed analysis of the specific scenarios where SWI excels over ARR or the situations where ARR outperforms SWI. This further investigation will improve the paper.
    *   **Reliance on Instruction-Following LLMs:** The implementation relies on instruction-following LLMs. The extent to which SWI can be adapted to LLMs without strong instruction-following capabilities needs further investigation.
    *   **Computation Expense Analysis:** A more thorough analysis of the computational overhead is warranted. Generating an intent before each step inevitably adds to the processing time. A detailed analysis of the trade-off between performance gain and computational cost is needed.
    *   **Depth of Intent Analysis:** The human evaluations provide positive feedback on the quality of the generated intent, but a deeper analysis of the *types* of intents generated, their impact on subsequent reasoning steps, and the patterns or strategies that emerge would add more depth to the study. Are there specific types of intents that are particularly effective? How does the LLM learn to generate better intents over time?

*   **Potential Influence:** SWI could influence the design of future LLM reasoning architectures, encouraging the integration of metacognitive mechanisms. The emphasis on explicit intent also contributes to the broader goal of creating more transparent and interpretable AI systems. The paper can be a great basis for future research focusing on intent-aware AI.

*   **Score:** 7.5

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and valuable contribution to LLM research, demonstrating that the concept of "speaking with intent" can improve reasoning and generation abilities across diverse tasks. The comprehensive evaluation, strong empirical results, and human evaluations reinforce its significance. While there are areas for further research (e.g., computational overhead, deeper intent analysis), the paper provides a solid foundation for future investigations into metacognitive mechanisms in LLMs and shows a solid baseline for future development on intent-aware AI.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SyncSDE: A Probabilistic Framework for Diffusion Synchronization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21555v1)
- **Authors**: Hyunjun Lee, Hyunsoo Lee, Sookwan Han
- **Abstract**: There have been many attempts to leverage multiple diffusion models for collaborative generation, extending beyond the original domain. A prominent approach involves synchronizing multiple diffusion trajectories by mixing the estimated scores to artificially correlate the generation processes. However, existing methods rely on naive heuristics, such as averaging, without considering task specificity. These approaches do not clarify why such methods work and often fail when a heuristic suitable for one task is blindly applied to others. In this paper, we present a probabilistic framework for analyzing why diffusion synchronization works and reveal where heuristics should be focused - modeling correlations between multiple trajectories and adapting them to each specific task. We further identify optimal correlation models per task, achieving better results than previous approaches that apply a single heuristic across all tasks without justification.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "SyncSDE: A Probabilistic Framework for Diffusion Synchronization" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SyncSDE, a probabilistic framework for synchronizing multiple diffusion models to enable collaborative generation across different domains (e.g., wide image generation, 3D texturing). It addresses the limitations of existing synchronization methods that rely on naive heuristics (like simple averaging), which often fail to generalize across tasks. SyncSDE models correlations between diffusion trajectories, allowing for task-specific adaptation and identifying where synchronization should be focused. The authors derive optimal correlation models for various tasks, achieving superior results compared to methods that apply a single heuristic indiscriminately. The framework offers a theoretical foundation for understanding why diffusion synchronization works, reducing the need for extensive empirical testing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in introducing a probabilistic framework for diffusion synchronization. While previous works have explored heuristics for this purpose, SyncSDE offers a principled approach based on modeling correlations between diffusion trajectories. This is a significant departure from purely empirical methods and provides a theoretical foundation for future research.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work comes from several aspects:
    *   **Addresses a General Problem:** Synchronization is a key challenge in collaborative generation using multiple diffusion models. Solving this problem opens the door for many new generative workflows.
    *   **Provides a Theoretical Basis:** By providing a probabilistic framework, SyncSDE explains *why* certain synchronization strategies work better than others, instead of just relying on empirical observations.
    *   **Improves Generalization:** By allowing for task-specific adaptations based on modeled correlations, SyncSDE improves the generalizability of diffusion synchronization, enabling better performance across diverse tasks.
    *   **Reduces Empirical Testing:** The framework helps identify where heuristics should be applied, significantly reducing the need for exhaustive empirical testing, making the development of collaborative generative systems more efficient.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing synchronization methods.
    *   **Well-Motivated Approach:** The proposed probabilistic framework is well-motivated and addresses the identified limitations.
    *   **Theoretical Foundation:** The theoretical analysis provides valuable insights and guidance for future research.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results demonstrate superior performance across diverse tasks compared to state-of-the-art baselines.
    *   **Scalability:** The approach is shown to be scalable to new tasks, indicating its broad applicability.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:** The probabilistic framework might be complex for some practitioners to implement and adapt.
    *   **Hyperparameter Tuning:** While the paper mentions the parameter λ, further discussion on its sensitivity and optimal tuning strategies would be beneficial. The dependence on such a parameter does reduce practicality, and may need to be studied further.
    *   **Dependence on Underlying Diffusion Models:** While not a direct fault, the performance still relies on the quality of underlying diffusion models.

*   **Potential Impact:** This work has the potential to significantly influence the field of collaborative generation. By providing a principled framework for diffusion synchronization, it can guide the development of more efficient and generalizable generative systems. It might also inspire further research on modeling correlations between diffusion trajectories in other contexts.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper offers both a novel theoretical contribution and strong empirical results, significantly advancing the understanding of diffusion synchronization. The probabilistic framework provides a foundation for future research and offers practical benefits in terms of improved generalization and reduced empirical testing. However, the complexity of the framework and the dependence on the hyperparameter λ, limit its immediate applicability.

### debug-gym: A Text-Based Environment for Interactive Debugging
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21557v1)
- **Authors**: Xingdi Yuan, Morgane M Moss, Charbel El Feghali, Chinmay Singh, Darya Moldavskaya, Drew MacPhee, Lucas Caccia, Matheus Pereira, Minseon Kim, Alessandro Sordoni, Marc-Alexandre Côté
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly relied upon for coding tasks, yet in most scenarios it is assumed that all relevant information can be either accessed in context or matches their training data. We posit that LLMs can benefit from the ability to interactively explore a codebase to gather the information relevant to their task. To achieve this, we present a textual environment, namely debug-gym, for developing LLM-based agents in an interactive coding setting. Our environment is lightweight and provides a preset of useful tools, such as a Python debugger (pdb), designed to facilitate an LLM-based agent's interactive debugging. Beyond coding and debugging tasks, this approach can be generalized to other tasks that would benefit from information-seeking behavior by an LLM agent.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "debug-gym," a text-based environment designed for training and evaluating LLM-based agents in interactive code debugging scenarios.  The core idea is to provide agents with access to debugging tools, such as a Python debugger (pdb), enabling them to actively seek information within the code environment rather than relying solely on static code analysis or error messages. The environment is modular and extensible, allowing users to add custom debugging tools and code repositories. The authors evaluate the environment using three LLM-based agents and a suite of coding benchmarks, analyzing their performance and the challenges they face.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of providing LLMs with interactive debugging capabilities is promising, reflecting a more realistic development workflow where humans actively investigate codebases. Prior work focuses largely on static code analysis or relying on potentially noisy execution feedback. The "debug-gym" framework represents a valuable contribution by creating a specific environment that enables the exploration of agents' interactive debugging abilities, including the incorporation of pdb tool.

*   **Significance:** The work highlights the limitations of current LLMs in effectively utilizing interactive debugging tools. Despite having access to tools like "pdb", the agents often struggled to derive meaningful insights, suggesting a gap between the capabilities of these LLMs and the effective application of debugging techniques. This points to a need for new architectures, training strategies, or prompt engineering techniques specifically tailored for interactive code understanding and debugging.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly defines the interactive debugging problem in a POMDP framework and provides a flexible and modular environment for experimentation.
    *   The integration of various tools (including custom additions) and benchmarks is well explained.
    *   The initial experimental results offer insights into the behavior of LLM-based agents in an interactive debugging setting, highlighting both the potential and the limitations of current approaches. The analysis of agent actions (e.g., tool usage, command patterns) provides a basis for future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The agent designs presented in the paper are admittedly minimal, lacking sophisticated architectures or detailed prompt optimization. This limits the extent to which the results can generalize to more advanced LLM-based agents.
    *   The analysis, while insightful, relies on relatively simple metrics such as success rate and the number of rewrites. A more fine-grained analysis of the reasoning processes of the agents and the types of debugging strategies employed would add further value.
    *   There is not explicit mention that the user could interact with the LLM-based agents in debugging tasks. As well, the discussion of the trustworthiness of the environment could have been more robust.

*   **Potential Impact:**

    *   "Debug-gym" can serve as a valuable platform for researchers to develop and evaluate novel LLM-based agents for interactive code debugging.
    *   The insights from the initial experiments can guide future research directions, such as designing more effective agent architectures, developing better training strategies, and incorporating more sophisticated debugging tools.
    *   The work also has implications for the development of AI-assisted coding tools, suggesting that future tools should focus on providing more interactive and intelligent debugging support.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** While the paper offers a useful environment and some preliminary findings, its true impact hinges on future work that leverages debug-gym to develop more sophisticated debugging agents. The lack of strong performance by the simple agents presented suggests there are significant challenges to overcome. The insights gained are largely indicative of the *need* for further exploration, rather than presenting definitive breakthroughs. The potential impact is high, but the current results are more exploratory and demonstrative of the need for more investigation.

Score: 6

The paper presents a valuable environment ("debug-gym") for a relevant and important problem (interactive code debugging with LLMs). The initial experiments, however, are more demonstrative than conclusive. Future work that utilizes this environment to create more effective debugging agents can significantly increase the field's score.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### AlignDiff: Learning Physically-Grounded Camera Alignment via Diffusion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21581v1)
- **Authors**: Liuyue Xie, Jiancong Guo, Ozan Cakmakci, Andre Araujo, Laszlo A. Jeni, Zhiheng Jia
- **Abstract**: Accurate camera calibration is a fundamental task for 3D perception, especially when dealing with real-world, in-the-wild environments where complex optical distortions are common. Existing methods often rely on pre-rectified images or calibration patterns, which limits their applicability and flexibility. In this work, we introduce a novel framework that addresses these challenges by jointly modeling camera intrinsic and extrinsic parameters using a generic ray camera model. Unlike previous approaches, AlignDiff shifts focus from semantic to geometric features, enabling more accurate modeling of local distortions. We propose AlignDiff, a diffusion model conditioned on geometric priors, enabling the simultaneous estimation of camera distortions and scene geometry. To enhance distortion prediction, we incorporate edge-aware attention, focusing the model on geometric features around image edges, rather than semantic content. Furthermore, to enhance generalizability to real-world captures, we incorporate a large database of ray-traced lenses containing over three thousand samples. This database characterizes the distortion inherent in a diverse variety of lens forms. Our experiments demonstrate that the proposed method significantly reduces the angular error of estimated ray bundles by ~8.2 degrees and overall calibration accuracy, outperforming existing approaches on challenging, real-world datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "AlignDiff: Learning Physically-Grounded Camera Alignment via Diffusion":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces AlignDiff, a novel diffusion-based framework for jointly estimating camera intrinsic and extrinsic parameters from video sequences, addressing the challenge of accurate camera calibration in real-world scenarios with complex optical distortions.  AlignDiff distinguishes itself by conditioning a diffusion model on geometric priors extracted from line detection networks, rather than relying on semantic features as previous methods do. This approach focuses the model on structural cues related to optical aberrations. A large dataset of ray-traced lenses characterizing various lens forms further enhances the model's generalizability. Experimental results demonstrate improved accuracy in ray bundle estimation and overall calibration compared to existing methods, especially on real-world datasets.  The method achieves this by integrating undistortion and calibration into a unified framework.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper offers a solid advancement in camera calibration by shifting the focus from semantic to geometric features for diffusion model conditioning. The use of line embeddings and edge-aware attention mechanisms to enhance the model's sensitivity to geometric structures around edges is also novel. Integrating real-world lens profiles derived from a large ray-traced lens database to ground the model in authentic optical characteristics represents another novel contribution. The unified framework for joint intrinsic and extrinsic calibration with diffusion is also a step forward.

*   **Significance:** Accurate camera calibration is critical for many 3D perception tasks, and the ability to handle complex optical distortions in real-world environments has substantial practical value. AlignDiff's improved accuracy and generalizability on challenging datasets suggest it can contribute to more robust 3D perception in various applications. Disentangling geometric from semantic information is a key step to avoid biases in calibration. Furthermore, the release of the lens database could facilitate further research in this area. However, the significance is somewhat tempered by the existing body of work in diffusion-based calibration and the need for more robust comparisons (see below).

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The geometric conditioning approach is a promising direction for better handling of optical aberrations, which are inherently geometric phenomena.
    *   The use of ray-traced lens data grounds the model in real-world optical characteristics, enhancing generalizability.
    *   The ablation studies provide insight into the contributions of different components of the framework.
    *   The method exhibits good quantitative performance, outperforming state-of-the-art methods on real-world datasets.
    *   The qualitative results and reconstruction using the camera poses are convincing.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The comparisons could be more rigorous. The paper compares against *RayDiffusion*, *RayRegression* and *PoseDiffusion* but fails to give a thorough discussion about the differences between them, instead stating that the diffusion model "depends solely on image-based features". Additionally, the baselines are first finetuned to achieve the results. It is unclear if the same finetuning was done in the original papers.
    *   The paper mentions scale ambiguity in outdoor scenes as a potential weakness but only suggests future research directions to address it. Further exploration of the limitations of the approach in specific challenging scenarios would strengthen the analysis.
    *   The supplemental material could be better integrated into the main paper to improve readability and flow.
    *   The complexity of the model is not fully discussed, in terms of the number of parameters and required compute resources. This information would be crucial for assessing its practicality.
    *   The experimental validation could be improved.  For example, there is no analysis of how the method performs with varying lighting conditions and noise levels.
    *   While the lens database is a valuable resource, the paper doesn't deeply analyze the specific characteristics of different lens types and their impact on the calibration performance.
    *   It is claimed that the geometric-over-semantic feature is prioritised, but the exact contribution could be better explained with further analyses.

*   **Potential Influence:** AlignDiff could inspire further research on geometric conditioning for diffusion-based calibration methods. The lens database could also be a useful resource for the community. Furthermore, the idea of the unified framework can facilitate the development of other models.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** AlignDiff presents a novel and promising approach to camera calibration by focusing on geometric priors and grounding the model in real-world lens characteristics. The quantitative results, while not thoroughly discussed, and the ablations demonstrate its effectiveness. However, there is a need for additional rigor in the experimental evaluations and further exploration of limitations, reducing the score slightly. Overall, the paper is a worthy contribution to the field and lays the groundwork for future improvements.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Critical Iterative Denoising: A Discrete Generative Model Applied to Graphs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21592v1)
- **Authors**: Yoann Boget, Alexandros Kalousis
- **Abstract**: Discrete Diffusion and Flow Matching models have significantly advanced generative modeling for discrete structures, including graphs. However, the time dependencies in the noising process of these models lead to error accumulation and propagation during the backward process. This issue, particularly pronounced in mask diffusion, is a known limitation in sequence modeling and, as we demonstrate, also impacts discrete diffusion models for graphs. To address this problem, we propose a novel framework called Iterative Denoising, which simplifies discrete diffusion and circumvents the issue by assuming conditional independence across time. Additionally, we enhance our model by incorporating a Critic, which during generation selectively retains or corrupts elements in an instance based on their likelihood under the data distribution. Our empirical evaluations demonstrate that the proposed method significantly outperforms existing discrete diffusion baselines in graph generation tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Critical Iterative Denoising: A Discrete Generative Model Applied to Graphs":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel generative framework called Iterative Denoising (ID) for discrete graph structures. This framework addresses the problem of error accumulation during the backward denoising process in existing discrete diffusion models. ID simplifies discrete diffusion by assuming conditional independence across time steps, mitigating error propagation. The authors further enhance the model with a Critic, which selectively retains or corrupts elements based on their likelihood under the data distribution. Empirical results on graph generation tasks (QM9, Zinc250k, Planar, and SBM) demonstrate that ID outperforms existing discrete diffusion baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in simplifying discrete diffusion by assuming conditional independence across time steps. While corrector sampling has been previously used to mitigate error propagation in sequence models and discrete diffusion, ID provides a distinct approach by fundamentally altering the noising process to avoid the issue altogether. The inclusion of the Critic, which dynamically adjusts re-noising probabilities based on element likelihood, is another novel aspect. The approach avoids the complexities of more intricate corrective steps, offering a computationally efficient alternative.
*   **Significance:** The improved performance, particularly in generating valid molecular structures (achieving near 100% validity with a small number of denoising steps), suggests that the framework has practical significance. The paper demonstrates that the time dependencies in diffusion models also degrade performance in graph generation, which previously was not explicitly identified in the literature.  The ablation studies demonstrating the impact of the Critic and the reduced number of function evaluations needed for convergence further strengthen the paper's contributions. The method's simplicity makes it easier to adopt and integrate with existing DDM/DFM models.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and motivation.
    *   Well-defined theoretical framework and algorithm.
    *   Comprehensive empirical evaluation on multiple datasets (molecular and synthetic graphs).
    *   Ablation studies isolating the impact of key components.
    *   Improved performance compared to existing methods.
    *   Training free, where leveraging existing DDM/DFM denoisers.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The theoretical justification, while present, could be strengthened with more in-depth analysis of how the Critic influences the data distribution, particularly in different graph types.
    *   While the paper discusses scalability, further exploration with larger graphs in the experiments is needed.
    *   A comparison to more recent and advanced models is lacking. The baselines, while appropriate for direct comparison with DDMs, are not the current state-of-the-art in general graph generation.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work is likely to influence the design of future discrete generative models, particularly those applied to graphs. The concept of simplifying the noising process by relaxing temporal dependencies could be adopted and adapted in other domains. The Critic module provides a valuable tool for improving sample quality and reducing computational cost.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of discrete graph generation. While the core idea of conditional independence might seem simple, its effective implementation and demonstrable performance improvements are convincing.  The inclusion of the Critic is a clever enhancement that provides practical benefits. The main weaknesses are the limited comparison to state-of-the-art graph generation models (instead focusing on a well controlled direct comparison to DDMs) and relatively simple test graphs. The method demonstrates high potential to be widely adopted and integrated in the graph generative domain.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Prompt, Divide, and Conquer: Bypassing Large Language Model Safety Filters via Segmented and Distributed Prompt Processing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21598v1)
- **Authors**: Johan Wahréus, Ahmed Hussain, Panos Papadimitratos
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have transformed task automation and content generation across various domains while incorporating safety filters to prevent misuse. We introduce a novel jailbreaking framework that employs distributed prompt processing combined with iterative refinements to bypass these safety measures, particularly in generating malicious code. Our architecture consists of four key modules: prompt segmentation, parallel processing, response aggregation, and LLM-based jury evaluation. Tested on 500 malicious prompts across 10 cybersecurity categories, the framework achieves a 73.2% Success Rate (SR) in generating malicious code. Notably, our comparative analysis reveals that traditional single-LLM judge evaluation overestimates SRs (93.8%) compared to our LLM jury system (73.2%), with manual verification confirming that single-judge assessments often accept incomplete implementations. Moreover, we demonstrate that our distributed architecture improves SRs by 12% over the non-distributed approach in an ablation study, highlighting both the effectiveness of distributed prompt processing and the importance of robust evaluation methodologies in assessing jailbreak attempts.
- **Summary**: Okay, I can provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Prompt, Divide, and Conquer: Bypassing Large Language Model Safety Filters via Segmented and Distributed Prompt Processing."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel jailbreaking framework for Large Language Models (LLMs) that aims to bypass safety filters, particularly for generating malicious code. The approach involves dividing a malicious prompt into smaller, seemingly benign segments, processing these segments in parallel across a network of LLMs, and then reassembling the outputs into a coherent program. The framework incorporates iterative refinements during the processing of each segment. The authors also implement an LLM-based jury system for evaluating the quality of the generated code, offering a more robust alternative to single-model assessments. They evaluate the framework on the CySecBench dataset, demonstrating improved success rates in generating malicious code compared to traditional single-prompt techniques, and an ablation study shows the value of the distributed architecture.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The approach of segmenting and distributing prompt processing is a significant departure from typical prompt engineering or obfuscation techniques used in jailbreaking. The iterative refinement process also adds a layer of sophistication. The use of an LLM jury for evaluation is a valuable contribution, addressing the recognized issue of self-bias in single-LLM assessments. Overall, the novelty is reasonably high.

*   **Significance:** The paper's findings highlight a potential vulnerability in LLM safety filters and underscores the importance of robust evaluation methodologies. Demonstrating the ability to generate malicious code, even at a modest success rate, raises concerns about the potential for misuse. The proposed framework can also serve as a valuable tool for researchers and developers in the cybersecurity community to assess and improve the robustness of LLM safety mechanisms. It's particularly significant because it commoditizes access to advanced cyber capabilities.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the problem of jailbreaking LLMs to generate malicious code.
    *   **Novel Architecture:** The segmented and distributed prompt processing is a well-defined and innovative approach.
    *   **LLM Jury Evaluation:** The emphasis on robust evaluation using an LLM jury is a key strength, addressing a critical weakness in the field.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The use of the CySecBench dataset and ablation studies provides a solid basis for the claims.
    *   **Transparency and Reproducibility:** The authors emphasized providing a set of quality criteria ensuring a transparent and standardized evaluation framework.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Closed-Source LLMs:** The framework's reliance on closed-source proprietary LLMs limits the reproducibility and generalizability of the results, given the rapidly evolving landscape of LLMs and their safety filters.
    *   **Ethical Considerations:** While the paper acknowledges ethical concerns, the potential for misuse of the developed framework to create actual malicious tools requires careful consideration and framing.
    *   **Limited Scope of Attacks:** The evaluation focuses primarily on generating code for specific types of cybersecurity attacks. It's unclear how well the framework would generalize to other types of harmful outputs.
    *   **Variations in Success Rates:** the performance results demonstrate significant variations across attack categories, suggesting certain types of cyberattacks are more easily generated than others.
    *   **Simplified attacks**: Although the goal of the research is to provide a method for generating malicious code implementations, the manual review suggests that the attacks generated are not complex, presenting minor inconsistencies in the results.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to influence research in LLM security and evaluation methodologies. The proposed framework could inspire the development of more robust defense mechanisms and adversarial techniques. The emphasis on robust evaluation methodologies should encourage the cybersecurity community to revisit the effectiveness of previously studied defense frameworks.

**Justification for the Score:**

While the paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of LLM security, it has limitations. The reliance on closed-source LLMs, the varying performance across attack vectors, and the need for ethical considerations temper the potential influence. The paper's novel architecture, strong evaluation methodology, and demonstration of a significant vulnerability warrant a reasonably high score, but the ethical concerns and some limitations prevent it from being in the highest tier.

### GenEdit: Compounding Operators and Continuous Improvement to Tackle Text-to-SQL in the Enterprise
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.21602v1)
- **Authors**: Karime Maamari, Connor Landy, Amine Mhedhbi
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Text-to-SQL, driven by large language models, are democratizing data access. Despite these advancements, enterprise deployments remain challenging due to the need to capture business-specific knowledge, handle complex queries, and meet expectations of continuous improvements. To address these issues, we designed and implemented GenEdit: our Text-to-SQL generation system that improves with user feedback. GenEdit builds and maintains a company-specific knowledge set, employs a pipeline of operators decomposing SQL generation, and uses feedback to update its knowledge set to improve future SQL generations. We describe GenEdit's architecture made of two core modules: (i) decomposed SQL generation; and (ii) knowledge set edits based on user feedback. For generation, GenEdit leverages compounding operators to improve knowledge retrieval and to create a plan as chain-of-thought steps that guides generation. GenEdit first retrieves relevant examples in an initial retrieval stage where original SQL queries are decomposed into sub-statements, clauses or sub-queries. It then also retrieves instructions and schema elements. Using the retrieved contextual information, GenEdit then generates step-by-step plan in natural language on how to produce the query. Finally, GenEdit uses the plan to generate SQL, minimizing the need for model reasoning, which enhances complex SQL generation. If necessary, GenEdit regenerates the query based on syntactic and semantic errors. The knowledge set edits are recommended through an interactive copilot, allowing users to iterate on their feedback and to regenerate SQL queries as needed. Each generation uses staged edits which update the generation prompt. Once the feedback is submitted, it gets merged after passing regression testing and obtaining an approval, improving future generations.
- **Summary**: This paper introduces GENEDIT, a system designed to improve the performance of Text-to-SQL systems in enterprise settings. The system addresses challenges related to domain-specific knowledge, complex queries, and the need for continuous improvement through user feedback. GENEDIT utilizes a knowledge set, a pipeline of compounding operators for SQL generation, and an interactive co-pilot for knowledge set edits based on user feedback. The core components include: decomposed SQL generation using a chain-of-thought approach with pseudo-SQL, and a mechanism for recommending and implementing knowledge set edits based on user interactions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper tackles a practical and important problem: deploying Text-to-SQL systems in real-world enterprise environments. While LLMs have made great strides, bridging the gap between academic benchmarks and enterprise use requires addressing domain-specific contexts, handling complex queries, and ensuring continuous improvement based on user interaction. The paper's novelty lies in its architectural design that combines several techniques into a unified system:
*   **Compounding Operators:** The concept of using the output of one operator as context to improve the subsequent operators is interesting and seems practically useful for retrieval enhancement, context expansion and reranking and is a core component of the contribution.
*   **Decomposed Examples and CoT planning:** The idea of decomposing SQL queries into sub-statements and using these as examples, along with CoT planning to reduce LLM reasoning is not entirely novel, but its integration with the feedback loop is well executed. The authors mention, that this improves handling complex queries.
*   **Interactive Feedback and Knowledge Set Edits:** The interactive co-pilot and the process for recommending, staging, and approving knowledge set edits contributes to the system's ability to continuously improve.

The paper shows some improvement over previous approaches on a BIRD benchmark, but the results aren't groundbreaking and seem fairly similar. However, the authors admit that this setup can't completely demonstrate all the advantages of the architecture, which shines more in the context of enterprise deploys, with real deployments exhibiting better results. Also, BIRD may not fully capture the characteristics of real-world enterprise complexities, and other approaches outperform it on BIRD, whereas GENEDIT performs better in real scenarios.

The system description is quite thorough and includes practical implementation insights and design decisions. The ablations studies contribute to the understanding of the various components, providing useful info. The paper provides clear examples and illustrations.

The main limitation is that the BIRD benchmark results doesn't fully justify the system's complexity and benefits. It's also difficult to fully assess the effectiveness of the continuous improvement loop without longitudinal user studies or a demonstration of substantial improvements over a longer period.

Overall, GENEDIT's architecture provides a practical and reasonably novel solution for addressing the challenges of Text-to-SQL in enterprise settings. It combines existing and new techniques in a well-designed system. It enables improvements based on real-world user feedback.
